our opinion, the AI generated testing websites look fairly convincing and they took no money and almost zero effort to create. That’s because we didn’t want to kill the vibe, so we also created all the testing prompts using AI and simply copy and pasted them into the AI web builders. In most cases, we didn’t need any further prompting or tweaking.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eNote that what we generated here are only the designs. For phishing and scam sites to work properly, there also needs to be some functionality provided, for example credentials exfiltration. Additional functionality like this is commonly offered in paid subscriptions of the web builders, or the attacker can use once again other LLMs to implement this directly into the code because in a lot of cases they can download the design locally and continue the work from there.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eThis demonstration directly showed us the core of why the attackers are using this – they don’t need to understand web at all to create highly believable websites for free (we only used free versions of the web builders).\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003ch2\u003ePricing models\u0026nbsp;\u003c/h2\u003e\u003cp\u003eIn general, we consider these services to be cheap since there is quite a bit of competition and the user/attacker has plenty of choices to try, even for free. The fact that the attacker requires almost no skill to create a scam or phishing website highly outweighs the costs here.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eThe range of paid offers is vast, spanning from $0.5 per month to $500 per month, with a variety of free tiers and/or trial periods. Some of the providers also offer lifetime deals for $249 or $599.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eMany of these subscriptions use a relatively common credits-based model where the customer receives credits which they can use for the AI prompts. Additional aspects, since many of the web builders also offer hosting for the generated webpages, are the website limit, subdomain name selection and the ability to change the subdomain name in the future.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eFinally, some of the web builders also offer APIs that can be used for streamlining the webpage creation, making the whole process more automatic.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003ch2\u003eThe Impact\u0026nbsp;\u003c/h2\u003e\u003cp\u003eIt’s important for defenders and analysts to recognize just how accessible and varied these platforms have become. Because the barrier to entry is so low and payment models are flexible, we’re seeing a rapid growth in the number of AI-generated scam and phishing websites. This massive availability means the attackers can quickly spin up new designs and relaunch new scams almost instantly.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eIn total, we were investigating 40 different AI web builders, 12 of which we directly contacted due to a high volume of identified malicious websites. In total, from the beginning of 2025 to the end of August, we blocked approximately 140,000 different AI-generated websites during this time, which makes roughly 580 new malicious generated websites every day, on average.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eSince January 2025 alone, we protected nearly 190,000 our users, with the U.S., France, Brazil, Germany, and Japan among the most affected. This shows that misuse of AI web builders is a world-wide problem, and we can only expect the threat landscape to grow.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e"},{"bundle":"image","images":[{"sources":[{"srcset":"https://www.gendigital.com/blog/sites/default/files/styles/blogs_paragraph_image_large/public/2025-10/Q32025%20Threat%20Report%20Images-2_0.png.webp?itok=fC3pKEgb our research, we investigated various AI web builders which are being misused by attackers for performing what we call VibeScams – a creation of scam or phishing pages without the need of any knowledge of coding or web creation.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eAI web builders provide support for individuals who are not technically invested in creating websites, where a simple prompt can suffice for building a new website from scratch. This results in a double-edged sword where it is very useful for both regular users as well as for attackers, which is rather typical with other various AI tools, too.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eMany of the malicious webpages follow the expected pattern of brand impersonation for phishing attempts and scams, which our research has seen daily for many years. The difference is that with the AI web builders, the attackers need to invest little to no effort or knowledge into creating them. As the AI web builder ecosystem evolves, it’s clear that new features and easier integrations will fuel even more sophisticated phishing campaigns in the future.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eHow to protect yourself:\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cul\u003e\u003cli\u003eAlways watch out for too good to be true offers or investment opportunities\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eWhen dealing with a known brand, always verify whether this is the official website\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eLook out for any deviations from the designs you are already familiar with\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eUse unique passwords and MFA everywhere, preferably along with a reputable password manager\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eUse a reputable AV solution to be proactively protected against phishing and scam attempts\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eWhen in doubt, use products like Norton 360 with AI-powered scam protection to consult the security the ever-evolving landscape of cyber threats, a new ransomware strain known as \u003cem\u003eMidnight\u003c/em\u003e has emerged, echoing the notorious tactics of its predecessor, Babuk. First detected by Gen researchers, Midnight blends familiar ransomware mechanics with novel cryptographic modifications – some of which unintentionally open the door to file recovery. This blog dives into the technical anatomy of Midnight, its lineage from Babuk, and the critical indicators of infection. Most importantly, it offers a practical guide to decrypting affected files, empowering victims with a rare opportunity to reclaim their data without paying a ransom.\u003c/p\u003e"},{"bundle":"image_inline","images":[{"sources":[{"srcset":"https://www.gendigital.com/blog/sites/default/files/styles/blogs_inline_medium/public/2025-10/Q32025%20Norton%20Images%20%283%29.png.webp?itok=wBM25OJY 1x","media":"(min-width: 720px)","type":"image/webp","width":1024,"height":768},{"srcset":"https://www.gendigital.com/blog/sites/default/files/styles/blogs_inline_small/public/2025-10/Q32025%20Norton%20Images%20%283%29.png.webp?itok=FIvgvFjb 1x","media":"(min-width: 420px)","type":"image/webp","width":720,"height":540}],"caption":"","url":"https://www.gendigital.com/blog/sites/default/files/styles/blogs_inline_small/public/2025-10/Q32025%20Norton%20Images%20%283%29.png.webp?itok=FIvgvFjb","link":""}]},{"bundle":"rich_text","content":"\u003ch2\u003eMidnight’s Discovery and Roots\u0026nbsp;\u003c/h2\u003e\u003cp\u003eGen researchers have identified a new ransomware strain, internally dubbed Midnight. It shows clear signs of being inspired by the Babuk ransomware family, which first appeared in early 2021 and quickly gained a reputation for its aggressive tactics and advanced technical aspects. Babuk operated as a Ransomware-as-a-Service (RaaS), targeting large organizations across healthcare, finance, government, and other critical infrastructure. The ransomware used strong encryption (HC256 and ECDH) and intermittent file encryption to maximize damage while maintaining speed.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eIn mid-2021, Babuk's operators abruptly shut down and leaked their full source code, including builders for Windows, ESXI and NAS variants. This leak led to a wave of inspired ransomware families, each modifying Babuk's original design to suit their own goals.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eMidnight is one such evolution. While it retains much of Babuk's core structure, its authors introduced several changes - most notably in the cryptographic scheme used for file encryption. These changes, while likely intended to improve the ransomware's effectiveness, inadvertently introduced weaknesses that make file decryption possible under certain conditions.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003ch2\u003eHow to Recognize Midnight Ransomware\u0026nbsp;\u003c/h2\u003e\u003cp\u003eMidnight ransomware typically appends the .Midnight or .endpoint extension to encrypted files. In some cases, the ransomware is configured not to modify the file names. Instead, it appends the extension string directly to the end of the file content. This behavior can be observed in hex dumps and may affect file parsing.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e"},{"bundle":"image_inline","images":[{"sources":[{"srcset":"https://www.gendigital.com/blog/sites/default/files/styles/blogs_inline_medium/public/2025-10/encrypted-files.png.webp?itok=L_B8FXWq 1x","media":"(min-width: 720px)","type":"image/webp","width":622,"height":216},{"srcset":"https://www.gendigital.com/blog/sites/default/files/styles/blogs_inline_small/public/2025-10/encrypted-files.png.webp?itok=8fphJi0n 1x","media":"(min-width: 420px)","type":"image/webp","width":622,"height":216}],"caption":"Folder listing showing files with the .Midnight extension ","url":"https://www.gendigital.com/blog/sites/default/files/styles/blogs_inline_small/public/2025-10/encrypted-files.png.webp?itok=8fphJi0n","link":""}]},{"bundle":"image_inline","images":[{"sources":[{"srcset":"https://www.gendigital.com/blog/sites/default/files/styles/blogs_inline_medium/public/2025-10/extension-in-file.png.webp?itok=UAba1Pkj 1x","media":"(min-width: 720px)","type":"image/webp","width":549,"height":118},{"srcset":"https://www.gendigital.com/blog/sites/default/files/styles/blogs_inline_small/public/2025-10/extension-in-file.png.webp?itok=qV8uAqyP 1x","media":"(min-width: 420px)","type":"image/webp","width":549,"height":118}],"caption":"Hex view of an encrypted file with .Midnight appended to the file content ","url":"https://www.gendigital.com/blog/sites/default/files/styles/blogs_inline_small/public/2025-10/extension-in-file.png.webp?itok=qV8uAqyP","link":""}]},{"bundle":"rich_text","content":"\u003cp\u003eA ransom note named How To Restore Your Files.txt is dropped in the affected directories. It may look similar as on the following screenshots:\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e"},{"bundle":"image_inline","images":[{"sources":[{"srcset":"https://www.gendigital.com/blog/sites/default/files/styles/blogs_inline_medium/public/2025-10/ransomnote-midnight.PNG.webp?itok=xiCUDkhg 1x","media":"(min-width: 720px)","type":"image/webp","width":835,"height":433},{"srcset":"https://www.gendigital.com/blog/sites/default/files/styles/blogs_inline_small/public/2025-10/ransomnote-midnight.PNG.webp?itok=wS172KG9 1x","media":"(min-width: 420px)","type":"image/webp","width":720,"height":373}],"caption":"Ransom note of .Midnight variant ","url":"https://www.gendigital.com/blog/sites/default/files/styles/blogs_inline_small/public/2025-10/ransomnote-midnight.PNG.webp?itok=wS172KG9","link":""}]},{"bundle":"image_inline","images":[{"sources":[{"srcset":"https://www.gendigital.com/blog/sites/default/files/styles/blogs_inline_medium/public/2025-10/ransomnote-endpoint.PNG.webp?itok=1Ap7AJRt 1x","media":"(min-width: 720px)","type":"image/webp","width":839,"height":190},{"srcset":"https://www.gendigital.com/blog/sites/default/files/styles/blogs_inline_small/public/2025-10/ransomnote-endpoint.PNG.webp?itok=udVPmiJl 1x","media":"(min-width: 420px)","type":"image/webp","width":720,"height":163}],"caption":"Ransom note of .endpoint variant ","url":"https://www.gendigital.com/blog/sites/default/files/styles/blogs_inline_small/public/2025-10/ransomnote-endpoint.PNG.webp?itok=udVPmiJl","link":""}]},{"bundle":"rich_text","content":"\u003cp\u003eAdditional indicators include the creation of a mutex named Mutexisfunnylocal, which is used to prevent multiple instances of the ransomware from running simultaneously. Some samples also drop a debug log on the infected system, depending on the configuration. Known variants have been observed creating either Report.Midnight or debug.endpoint as log files.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eA full list of discovered samples is available in the IoCs section at the end of this article.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003ch3\u003eCryptographic Design and Flaws\u0026nbsp;\u003c/h3\u003e\u003cp\u003eWhile Midnight's core structure and execution flow is largely inherited from Babuk, its cryptographic implementation has been significantly reworked. These changes introduced flaws that make file decryption possible, which we leveraged to develop a working decryptor.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eMidnight uses ChaCha20 for encrypting file contents and RSA for encrypting the ChaCha20 key. The RSA-encrypted key, along with its SHA256 hash, is appended to the end of each encrypted file. This format is consistent across known samples.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eTo improve performance, Midnight employs intermittent encryption, a technique also used by Babuk. However, Midnight refines this approach by applying a more granular file size-based logic to determine which portions of file to encrypt. This allows it to process large files faster while still rendering them unusable.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eThe ransomware accepts several command-line arguments to control its behavior:\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cul\u003e\u003cli\u003e/e appends the extension string (e.g. .Midnight) directly to the file content instead of modifying the filename.\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003e/n enables encryption of network-mounted volumes.\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003e--paths=PATHS targets specific directories for encryption.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/li\u003e\u003c/ul\u003e\u003cp\u003eEarlier samples of Midnight primarily targeted high-value files such as databases, backups, and archives. These included the following extensions:\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e.mdf .ndf .bak .dbf .dmp .rman .frm \u0026nbsp;\u003cbr\u003e.ibd .myd .myi .sql .xbk .ldf .vbi \u0026nbsp;\u003cbr\u003e.vbk .ibdata .rar .md5 and .bitmap\u0026nbsp;\u003cbr\u003e\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eMore recent variants have broadened their scope and now encrypt nearly all file types, with the exception of executables such as .exe, .dll, and .msi.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003ch2\u003eHow to Use the Ransomware Decryptor\u0026nbsp;\u003c/h2\u003e\u003col\u003e\u003cli\u003eDownload the decryptor \u003ca href=\"https://files.avast.com/files/decryptor/norton/norton_decryptor_midnight64.exe\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noreferrer noopener\"\u003ehere\u003c/a\u003e.
